People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Um… I guess work is what pushed me to come in. I’m a waitress, and I’ve been having these moments during service where I kind of… freeze. Not for long, maybe, but enough that people notice.

It just feels hard to shake.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and one of the regular customers are watching how I ring things in and leaving little signs on the tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired, even when my husband says it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. realize nobody else heard it.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: What scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service or doing things out of order, like standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key and repeating table numbers instead of closing checks.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I don’t really go for beach walks, bake, garden, or write in my journal much anymore, and when my husband tries to talk with me I mostly just sit there because it feels like I can’t get myself to care or start anything.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’m close to losing the shift-lead opportunity because my mistakes and confusion have disrupted service and closing, and at home my husband says I’m barely present with him anymore.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months, since I was asked to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The clearer voices and firm beliefs about coworkers and customers targeting me have been happening for about four months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something specific when you think someone is watching you or trying to find mistakes in your work to make yourself feel better or safer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not something specific like I have to tap something or say something, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI might look back at the ticket or the register again, or kind of keep my head down and try to act normal, but that just feels like… trying to make sure I’m doing my job right. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess work is what pushed me to come in. I’m a waitress, and I’ve been having these moments during service where I kind of… freeze. Not for long, maybe, but enough that people notice.\n\nI was doing things out of order, too—like I’d be standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and instead of closing checks I’d just be repeating table numbers to myself. It scared my coworkers, I think. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been happening enough that they thought I should talk to someone. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous before these freezing moments happen?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’m, like, panicking beforehand or anything. Maybe I get a little tense because it’s busy and I don’t want to mess up, but I haven’t really noticed a clear nervous feeling right before I freeze. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really sad, exactly. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, and embarrassed when people notice I’m not doing things right, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling down all the time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there is something I keep thinking about, but I don’t know if it counts.\n\nAt work I keep feeling like maybe a couple of the other servers, and this one regular customer, are… watching how I ring things in. Like they’re looking for mistakes. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.

But it kind of just pops in, especially at work. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.

Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim.   {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient who has come to a psychiatric clinic with Schizophrenia. perception.\n  manifestation: Sometimes during closing or when I’m home in the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice say my name or tell me I’m doing the count wrong, clear enough that I answer before I inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: What scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service or doing things out of order, like standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key and repeating table numbers instead of closing checks.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I don’t really go for beach walks, bake, garden, or write in my journal much anymore, and when my husband tries to talk with me I mostly just sit there because it feels like I can’t get myself to care or start anything.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’m close to losing the shift-lead opportunity because my mistakes and confusion have disrupted service and closing, and at home my husband says I’m barely present with him anymore.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months, since I was asked to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The clearer voices and firm beliefs about coworkers and customers targeting me have been happening for about four months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.\n\nBut it kind of just pops in, especially at work. Like if a couple of the servers are near the register, or one of the regular customers is watching, I start feeling like they’re looking at how I ring things in. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. But once the thought is there, I don’t feel like I fully control it. It just stays there until I check or get through the shift. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut it has been coming home with me more than I’d like. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. I was trying really hard for that, so… yeah, I guess it is affecting things outside of just the moment at work. Does that answer it okay?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. I know there isn’t, like, solid proof, and I don’t want to sound paranoid or unfair to them.

But when it’s happening, it feels like the servers are watching how I ring things in, and that one regular customer too. And then if there are little weird things on the tickets, my brain goes to, “See, they’re leaving signs,” like they’re trying to show I made mistakes and maybe get me fired.

My husband tells me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do try to believe that. But I can’t fully shake the feeling that maybe it’s real. Is that… is that what you’re asking?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F41.0, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess work is what pushed me to come in. I’m a waitress, and I’ve been having these moments during service where I kind of… freeze. Not for long, maybe, but enough that people notice.\n\nI was doing things out of order, too—like I’d be standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and instead of closing checks I’d just be repeating table numbers to myself. It scared my coworkers, I think. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been happening enough that they thought I should talk to someone. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous before these freezing moments happen?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’m, like, panicking beforehand or anything. Maybe I get a little tense because it’s busy and I don’t want to mess up, but I haven’t really noticed a clear nervous feeling right before I freeze. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really sad, exactly. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, and embarrassed when people notice I’m not doing things right, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling down all the time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there is something I keep thinking about, but I don’t know if it counts.\n\nAt work I keep feeling like maybe a couple of the other servers, and this one regular customer, are… watching how I ring things in. Like they’re looking for mistakes. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something specific when you think someone is watching you or trying to find mistakes in your work to make yourself feel better or safer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not something specific like I have to tap something or say something, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI might look back at the ticket or the register again, or kind of keep my head down and try to act normal, but that just feels like… trying to make sure I’m doing my job right. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean… I do feel better after I check, because then I can tell myself, okay, I didn’t mess that up.\n\nBut I don’t know if it’s like I have to do it, exactly. It’s more like if I don’t check, it stays in my head and I keep wondering about it. So I’ll look again just to quiet that down a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to make it sound like some big thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.\n\nBut it kind of just pops in, especially at work. Like if a couple of the servers are near the register, or one of the regular customers is watching, I start feeling like they’re looking at how I ring things in. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. But once the thought is there, I don’t feel like I fully control it. It just stays there until I check or get through the shift. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut it has been coming home with me more than I’d like. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. And then if there are little weird things on the tickets, my brain goes to, “See, they’re leaving signs,” like they’re trying to show I made mistakes and maybe get me fired.\n\nMy husband tells me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do try to believe that. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. I was trying really hard for that, so… yeah, I guess it is affecting things outside of just the moment at work. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there is something I keep thinking about, but I don’t know if it counts.\n\nAt work I keep feeling like maybe a couple of the other servers, and this one regular customer, are… watching how I ring things in. Like they’re looking for mistakes. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something specific when you think someone is watching you or trying to find mistakes in your work to make yourself feel better or safer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not something specific like I have to tap something or say something, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI might look back at the ticket or the register again, or kind of keep my head down and try to act normal, but that just feels like… trying to make sure I’m doing my job right. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean… I do feel better after I check, because then I can tell myself, okay, I didn’t mess that up.\n\nBut I don’t know if it’s like I have to do it, exactly. It’s more like if I don’t check, it stays in my head and I keep wondering about it. So I’ll look again just to quiet that down a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to make it sound like some big thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.\n\nBut it kind of just pops in, especially at work. Like if a couple of the servers are near the register, or one of the regular customers is watching, I start feeling like they’re looking at how I ring things in. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. But once the thought is there, I don’t feel like I fully control it. It just stays there until I check or get through the shift. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut it has been coming home with me more than I’d like. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. I was trying really hard for that, so… yeah, I guess it is affecting things outside of just the moment at work. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. I know there isn’t, like, solid proof, and I don’t want to sound paranoid or unfair to them.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it feels like the servers are watching how I ring things in, and that one regular customer too. And then if there are little weird things on the tickets, my brain goes to, “See, they’re leaving signs,” like they’re trying to show I made mistakes and maybe get me fired.\n\nMy husband tells me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do try to believe that. But I can’t fully shake the feeling that maybe it’s real. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over because you feel like you have to, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I do replay things in my head sometimes, like whether I rang something in wrong, but I don’t have, like, a set thing I have to do over and over to make it feel okay. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nMaybe I’m misunderstanding, though—do you mean like checking things repeatedly or rituals? I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess work is what pushed me to come in. I’m a waitress, and I’ve been having these moments during service where I kind of… freeze. Not for long, maybe, but enough that people notice.\n\nI was doing things out of order, too—like I’d be standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and instead of closing checks I’d just be repeating table numbers to myself. It scared my coworkers, I think. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been happening enough that they thought I should talk to someone. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous before these freezing moments happen?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’m, like, panicking beforehand or anything. Maybe I get a little tense because it’s busy and I don’t want to mess up, but I haven’t really noticed a clear nervous feeling right before I freeze. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really sad, exactly. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, and embarrassed when people notice I’m not doing things right, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling down all the time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there is something I keep thinking about, but I don’t know if it counts.\n\nAt work I keep feeling like maybe a couple of the other servers, and this one regular customer, are… watching how I ring things in. Like they’re looking for mistakes. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something specific when you think someone is watching you or trying to find mistakes in your work to make yourself feel better or safer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not something specific like I have to tap something or say something, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI might look back at the ticket or the register again, or kind of keep my head down and try to act normal, but that just feels like… trying to make sure I’m doing my job right. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean… I do feel better after I check, because then I can tell myself, okay, I didn’t mess that up.\n\nBut I don’t know if it’s like I have to do it, exactly. It’s more like if I don’t check, it stays in my head and I keep wondering about it. So I’ll look again just to quiet that down a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to make it sound like some big thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.\n\nBut it kind of just pops in, especially at work. Like if a couple of the servers are near the register, or one of the regular customers is watching, I start feeling like they’re looking at how I ring things in. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. But once the thought is there, I don’t feel like I fully control it. It just stays there until I check or get through the shift. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut it has been coming home with me more than I’d like. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. I was trying really hard for that, so… yeah, I guess it is affecting things outside of just the moment at work. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. I know there isn’t, like, solid proof, and I don’t want to sound paranoid or unfair to them.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it feels like the servers are watching how I ring things in, and that one regular customer too. And then if there are little weird things on the tickets, my brain goes to, “See, they’re leaving signs,” like they’re trying to show I made mistakes and maybe get me fired.\n\nMy husband tells me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do try to believe that. But I can’t fully shake the feeling that maybe it’s real. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over because you feel like you have to, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I do replay things in my head sometimes, like whether I rang something in wrong, but I don’t have, like, a set thing I have to do over and over to make it feel okay. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nMaybe I’m misunderstanding, though—do you mean like checking things repeatedly or rituals? Because I don’t think I really do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing anything to try to prevent the servers or the regular customer from supposedly finding mistakes or getting you fired?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in some big way. I’m not, like, confronting them or anything.\n\nBut I do catch myself trying to be extra careful when I ring things in, especially if those servers are nearby or that regular customer is sitting where they can see. I’ll look at the ticket and think, okay, don’t give them anything to point to. And if there’s some little weird note or mistake on the ticket, I start wondering if it’s one of those “signs” again, like they’re trying to show I messed up.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant stuff, and I’m trying not to make a whole thing out of it. But… yeah, I guess I do act like it might be true sometimes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing anything to try to neutralize or undo the potential harm you think the servers or the regular customer might be trying to cause you?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I do anything like that, no. Not to, like, undo it or cancel it out.\n\nI might try to be more careful, like I said, but I’m not doing some specific thing to make the harm not happen, if that makes sense. I don’t really have a ritual or anything. I mostly just get worried and try to keep working normally.\n\nIs that an okay answer? I’m not sure if I’m understanding “neutralize” the right way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I’d like to say it’s facts, because it feels pretty clear to me when it’s happening. Like, there’ll be a weird note on a ticket or some little mistake, and if those same servers are around, or that regular customer is watching, it really feels like they’re pointing it out on purpose—like they’re building a case or something to get me fired.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess work is what pushed me to come in. I’m a waitress, and I’ve been having these moments during service where I kind of… freeze. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous before these freezing moments happen?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’m, like, panicking beforehand or anything. I mean, I get frustrated with myself, and embarrassed when people notice I’m not doing things right, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling down all the time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there is something I keep thinking about, but I don’t know if it counts.\n\nAt work I keep feeling like maybe a couple of the other servers, and this one regular customer, are… watching how I ring things in. Like they’re looking for mistakes. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something specific when you think someone is watching you or trying to find mistakes in your work to make yourself feel better or safer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not something specific like I have to tap something or say something, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI might look back at the ticket or the register again, or kind of keep my head down and try to act normal, but that just feels like… trying to make sure I’m doing my job right. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean… I do feel better after I check, because then I can tell myself, okay, I didn’t mess that up.\n\nBut I don’t know if it’s like I have to do it, exactly. It’s more like if I don’t check, it stays in my head and I keep wondering about it. So I’ll look again just to quiet that down a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to make it sound like some big thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.\n\nBut it kind of just pops in, especially at work. Like if a couple of the servers are near the register, or one of the regular customers is watching, I start feeling like they’re looking at how I ring things in. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. But once the thought is there, I don’t feel like I fully control it. It just stays there until I check or get through the shift. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut it has been coming home with me more than I’d like. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. I was trying really hard for that, so… yeah, I guess it is affecting things outside of just the moment at work. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. I know there isn’t, like, solid proof, and I don’t want to sound paranoid or unfair to them.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it feels like the servers are watching how I ring things in, and that one regular customer too. And then if there are little weird things on the tickets, my brain goes to, “See, they’re leaving signs,” like they’re trying to show I made mistakes and maybe get me fired.\n\nMy husband tells me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do try to believe that. But I can’t fully shake the feeling that maybe it’s real. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over because you feel like you have to, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Like they’re looking for mistakes. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, like a note or a mark or something rung in wrong, I start thinking it’s a sign that they’re trying to show I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant chaos and little mistakes, and I know that sounds more reasonable. I’m trying not to make a big deal of it. But the thought kind of sticks, and I keep going back over the tickets in my head. Does that make sense, or does that sound… too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the other servers and the regular customer are actually watching you and trying to find mistakes, or do you think that's just your imagination?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s probably my imagination, because I know that sounds like the healthier answer… and my husband keeps saying it’s just normal work stuff.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels possible. Like, a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then if I see a weird note or something off on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign they’re building a case or trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know that sounds kind of extreme when I say it out loud. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that the servers or the regular customer might be plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, I haven’t caught them doing anything outside of work or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more when I’m at the restaurant, with the tickets and the register. I start feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular are watching how I ring things in, and then little weird marks or mistakes on tickets feel like signs they’re trying to prove I’m messing up so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I’m trying to keep that in mind. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you and trying to find mistakes is definitely true, or is it just a possibility that you're considering?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely true… I know that would be a lot to claim. I’m trying to keep it as just a possibility in my mind.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels more real than I want it to. Like I’ll see something on a ticket, or notice them looking over, and I start thinking, okay, maybe they are watching how I ring things in and trying to show I’m making mistakes so I’ll get fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do hear that. I just… I have a hard time fully letting it go. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety about being watched or making mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have rituals, exactly. Not like… anything strange, I don’t think.\n\nI do check things more than I probably need to, like making sure I rang something in right or looking over the ticket again, but I feel like that’s also kind of part of the job, right? I’m just trying not to make mistakes.\n\nI guess if you mean something I feel I have to do in a certain way to make the anxiety go away, I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something specific when you think someone is watching you or trying to find mistakes in your work to make yourself feel better or safer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not something specific like I have to tap something or say something, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI might look back at the ticket or the register again, or kind of keep my head down and try to act normal, but that just feels like… trying to make sure I’m doing my job right. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question—I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking things multiple times is something you have to do to feel okay about your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean… I do feel better after I check, because then I can tell myself, okay, I didn’t mess that up.\n\nBut I don’t know if it’s like I have to do it, exactly. It’s more like if I don’t check, it stays in my head and I keep wondering about it. So I’ll look again just to quiet that down a little.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to make it sound like some big thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can try to push it away, and sometimes I tell myself I’m probably just being too sensitive.\n\nBut it kind of just pops in, especially at work. Like if a couple of the servers are near the register, or one of the regular customers is watching, I start feeling like they’re looking at how I ring things in. And then if there’s something weird on a ticket, I’ll think maybe it’s like… a little sign that they’re trying to show I messed up or get me in trouble.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy and people make mistakes, and I do try to listen to that. But once the thought is there, I don’t feel like I fully control it. It just stays there until I check or get through the shift. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want those thoughts to come in, and I know it probably sounds kind of strange.\n\nIt’s like I’ll just be trying to ring things in, and then suddenly I notice a couple of the servers nearby or that regular customer watching, and my mind goes right to, “They’re checking me,” or “They’re trying to catch a mistake.” And then if there’s something off or messy on a ticket, I start thinking maybe it’s a little sign, like proof they’re trying to show I messed up and get me fired.\n\nI know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, and I do try to take that in. I’m not trying to be difficult. But yes, it does feel like it comes into my head without me wanting it to.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being watched and making mistakes affecting your ability to do things outside of work?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nBut it has been coming home with me more than I’d like. My husband says I’m kind of barely there with him lately, like I’m sitting there but still thinking about work or replaying things in my head. I don’t like that he feels that way.\n\nAnd at work… I guess it’s not just in my head anymore. The mistakes and me getting confused have messed up service and closing a few times, and I’m worried I’m close to losing the chance to be shift lead. I was trying really hard for that, so… yeah, I guess it is affecting things outside of just the moment at work. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the idea of the servers and the regular customer watching you to find mistakes is something that might be true, even if there's no real evidence for it?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess part of me does think it could be true. I know there isn’t, like, solid proof, and I don’t want to sound paranoid or unfair to them.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it feels like the servers are watching how I ring things in, and that one regular customer too. And then if there are little weird things on the tickets, my brain goes to, “See, they’re leaving signs,” like they’re trying to show I made mistakes and maybe get me fired.\n\nMy husband tells me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and I do try to believe that. But I can’t fully shake the feeling that maybe it’s real. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over because you feel like you have to, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I do replay things in my head sometimes, like whether I rang something in wrong, but I don’t have, like, a set thing I have to do over and over to make it feel okay. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nMaybe I’m misunderstanding, though—do you mean like checking things repeatedly or rituals? Because I don’t think I really do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing anything to try to prevent the servers or the regular customer from supposedly finding mistakes or getting you fired?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in some big way. I’m not, like, confronting them or anything.\n\nBut I do catch myself trying to be extra careful when I ring things in, especially if those servers are nearby or that regular customer is sitting where they can see. I’ll look at the ticket and think, okay, don’t give them anything to point to. And if there’s some little weird note or mistake on the ticket, I start wondering if it’s one of those “signs” again, like they’re trying to show I messed up.\n\nI know my husband says it’s probably just normal restaurant stuff, and I’m trying not to make a whole thing out of it. But… yeah, I guess I do act like it might be true sometimes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing anything to try to neutralize or undo the potential harm you think the servers or the regular customer might be trying to cause you?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I do anything like that, no. Not to, like, undo it or cancel it out.\n\nI might try to be more careful, like I said, but I’m not doing some specific thing to make the harm not happen, if that makes sense. I don’t really have a ritual or anything. I mostly just get worried and try to keep working normally.\n\nIs that an okay answer? I’m not sure if I’m understanding “neutralize” the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you to find mistakes is based on facts or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say it’s facts, because it feels pretty clear to me when it’s happening. Like, there’ll be a weird note on a ticket or some little mistake, and if those same servers are around, or that regular customer is watching, it really feels like they’re pointing it out on purpose—like they’re building a case or something to get me fired.\n\nBut I know that probably sounds… maybe like I’m reading too much into it. My husband keeps saying restaurants are just messy and tickets get weird sometimes. So I guess I don’t have solid proof, no. It’s more like a feeling that feels factual, if that makes sense. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're mentally repeating certain words, phrases, or numbers to yourself when you're not trying to?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like certain words or numbers getting stuck and repeating on purpose or against my will.\n\nI mean, I might replay a situation in my head a little, like anyone does, but I haven’t noticed myself repeating phrases or counting or anything like that. If that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that the servers and the regular customer are watching you to find mistakes is something that you believe is definitely true, regardless of what others say?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to say “definitely” because I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, too strong. And I’m trying to be fair.\n\nBut when I’m at work and it happens, yes, it feels true to me. Like when I see the little notes on the tickets or the way those servers are around when I’m ringing things in, it feels like they’re watching for mistakes and trying to have proof against me. And even when my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, I still have a hard time believing it’s nothing.\n\nSo… I guess not “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it, but inside it feels pretty true. Is that answering it right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
